Observations

1-Amul Biscuits — Sales are very low on this beat. Price is on the higher side compared to local and other branded biscuits. Only one outlet near First Link Road orders it regularly. Most consumers here are price-sensitive and prefer cheaper alternatives. Those who do buy are either loyal Amul customers or higher-income consumers.

2-Amul Organic Atta — Getting a good response from consumers. Demand seems better compared to other products in the same category.

3-Amul Lassi — Stores have demand for it but today the distributor did not have stock. This led to unfulfilled orders and lost sales. Stock needs to be arranged at the earliest.

4-Amul Paneer — Store 1 is not willing to keep it due to the high price. Consumer demand for it at that price point is low in this area.

5- Non-ordering Outlets  — 4 outlets did not place any order today. The reason given was slow market movement — consumers are not purchasing much currently, so existing stock is sufficient and they did not need a refill. ‎<This message was edited>

[23/05/26, 6:12:44 PM] Biprashi Nath Corporate: Distributor Name :- Sengupta Agencies 

Beat Name :- Hailakandi Road

Intern Name :- Biprarshi Nath 

Salesman Name :- Nehal Paul

Total no. of Outlet :- 34

No. Of outlets Visited :- 34

No. Of Outlets from where Order received :- 25

Market Observations:-

1. Due to unfavorable weather conditions, overall market movement was low today. Retailers reported fewer customer footfalls, delayed purchasing behavior, and concerns regarding timely product delivery and partial payments.

2. Strong demand and visibility were observed for products such as Amul T-Special, Amul Gold, Milkmaid, Mithai Mate, Fresh Cream, Kaju Katri, Amul Ice Cream, Cheese, Butter Cookies, and Butter Nankhatai across multiple outlets.

3. Retailers reported high demand for small-value SKUs such as ₹10 and 100g/200g packs of Amulya, T-Special, and Amul Spray, indicating higher preference for affordable and low-quantity packs among consumers.

4. Certain products showed demand but were unavailable due to stock shortages, especially Amul Atta 1kg (₹50 pack) and Amul Butter 50g. Retailers mentioned that stock availability could improve sales performance.

5. Some products such as 800g Tin Paneer were considered expensive by customers, leading to lower demand. In the biscuit category, competitor brands like Mother Dairy and Bisk Farm were observed to have stronger visibility and sales in several outlets compared to Amul biscuits.
[23/05/26, 6:19:35 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: ‎This message was deleted.
[23/05/26, 6:30:01 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Distributor Name: Sengupta Agencies
Beat Name: National Highway 
Salesman Name: Sushil Das
Intern Name: Tahir Choudhury
Total No. of Outlets: 18
Outlets Visited: 18
Orders Received: 15
Order Conversion Rate: 83.3%

Observations

1- Amul Lassi
Lassi continues to have strong demand in the market. Similar to previous observations, retailers showed interest in the product, but stock availability was limited. Because of this, full demand could not be converted into sales, leading to missed sales opportunities.

2- Impact of Rain on Sales
Due to the rain, market activity was slower today. Customer footfall was lower than usual, which affected product movement and order generation. Some retailers were less willing to place fresh orders because existing stock was moving slowly.

3- Amul Biscuits
Demand for Amul biscuits was noticeably better in the National Highway beat compared to the Sonai Road beat. Even outlets that already had biscuit stock, such as Store 8, placed repeat orders, showing strong product movement in that area. Compared to Sonai Road, customers here appear to have better purchasing power, which may be supporting higher demand. At the same time, stock shortages are creating retailer frustration, as some shopkeepers are unable to meet customer demand even after advertising efforts.

4- Amul Paneer
Retailer feedback on Amul tinned paneer was mixed. One retailer mentioned that the product quality is good, but the price is considered high for the local market. The same retailer also shared that some competing paneer brands are preferred by customers because of taste, which affects Amul paneer sales in certain outlets.

5- Amul Fresh Cream
Amul Fresh Cream is seeing good demand in the market. However, because the product has a shorter shelf life, retailers may avoid ordering larger quantities due to the risk of unsold stock getting spoiled.

6- Consumer Buying Pattern by Area
A clear difference in customer buying behaviour was observed between different areas. Products like Amul biscuits performed better in locations with comparatively higher-income customers, while more price-sensitive areas showed weaker demand for premium-priced products.

7- Non-ordering Outlets
Three outlets did not place orders today. The likely reasons were slow market movement due to rain and sufficient existing stock with retailers.

8- Overall Product Movement
Among the products ordered today, Amul Tazza and Amul Gold showed strong movement across multiple outlets, indicating consistent demand for daily dairy consumption products. Beverage products like Amul Kool and Amul Tru Litchi also received orders, showing reasonable market acceptance.

[23/05/26, 7:43:54 PM] Hrishab Dutta Majumdar NIT Agartala: Distributor Name - Raunak Enterprise 

Beat Name - Rangirkhari to Sonai

Intern Name - Hrishab Dutta Mazumder 

Salesman Name - Saurab Nath


No. Of outlets Visited - 20

No. Of Outlets from where Order received - 14


Market Observations:

1. Due to unfavorable weather conditions, overall market movement remained low today. 

2. Retailers reported reduced customer footfall, which resulted in lower order generation across several outlets.

3. In some areas, retailers expressed concern regarding electricity issues, which affected the sales of frozen products and reduced retailer confidence in stocking higher quantities of ice cream products.

4. High demand was observed for ₹10 ice cream SKUs across multiple outlets. However, stock was unavailable in several cases, which affected potential sales opportunities.

5. The ₹20 Choco Bar showed average market demand, but retailers reported stock unavailability in certain outlets, leading to missed sales opportunities.
[23/05/26, 7:49:30 PM] Aritrika NIT Agartala: Distributor Name - Raunak Enterprise 

Beat Name - Rangirkhari to Sonai

Intern Name - Aritrika Dihidar

Salesman Name - Saurab Nath

No. Of outlets Visited - 20

No. Of Outlets from where Order received - 14

Observations:

During the market visit, it was observed that due to lower temperatures and continuous rainfall, many areas were facing electricity issues. Because of this, several shop’s refrigerators and freezers were not functioning properly, which directly affected the storage and sales of ice cream products. Retailers were therefore placing comparatively lower orders for ice cream. 

In one of the shops visited, the Amul refrigerator itself was not working, due to which the shop owner did not place any order for ice cream products.

Many retailers mentioned that ₹10 ice cream products still had strong customer demand. However, due to limited supply availability of ₹10 SKUs, the retailer demand could not be fulfilled properly.

Along with ice cream products, most of the shops showed good demand for dairy products from the dairy section. Retailers also reported demand for frozen snack items such as pizza and French fries, indicating growing customer interest in frozen food categories beyond ice cream.
[23/05/26, 8:57:33 PM] Tãnishã NIT MBA: Key observations

1. *Amul Gold, Amul Taaza and Amulya* 
Strong demand was observed for daily consumption dairy products such as Amul Gold, Amul Taaza, and Amulya in multiple outlets almost in 80-90% outlets from which order was taken in college road and shashan road beats.
Variety of SKUs of these products were ordered. 

2. *Amul kool*
Amul kool proved to be One of the most demanding drinks in college road area although less than amul taaza and amul gold. 

3. *Amul lassi*
Retailers reported that the sale of amul lassi is going quite well. However, stock shortage was observed for this product. 

4. *Cheese and Paneer* 
Although the ordering level of these products were not remarkable yet the outlets which sell these products prefer to restock them once run out. Compared to Shashan road area (such as sunil sharkar lane), the sale of cheese and paneer was relatively higher in college road area and subhash nagar area

5. *Fresh cream*

The order for this product was low (notably at Radhe shyam enterprise)

In some outlets, retailers expressed concern regarding inconsistent electricity supply, which affected freezer performance and reduced confidence in stocking larger quantities of frozen products like ice cream.

[25/05/26, 6:36:24 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Distributor Name: Sengupta Agencies
Beat Name: Sarat Pally
Salesman Name: Sushil Das
Intern Name: Tahir Choudhury
Total No. of Outlets: 22
Outlets Visited: 22
Orders Received: 14
Order Conversion Rate: 63.6%

Field Observations-

1- Amul Tazza & Amul Gold
Amul Tazza and Amul Gold showed strong demand across multiple outlets in the Sarat Pally beat. Among all products, Amul Tazza 200 ml had the highest movement, indicating strong daily consumption demand and preference for smaller, affordable pack sizes. These products remained the key drivers of sales in this market.

2- Amulya Milk Powder
Amulya milk powder showed good demand in the market. Retailers indicated movement for ₹10 strips as well as 100 g and 200 g packs. This category appears to have strong growth potential and can be pushed further in suitable outlets.

3- Amul Kool
Retailer feedback suggested that Amul Kool has good market demand in this beat. Better stock availability and stronger distribution support could improve sales performance in this category.

4- Amul Tru Range
The Amul Tru beverage range showed average market performance. Among the variants, Mango performed better, while Litchi showed moderate movement. Other variants had weaker response. Retailers appear more comfortable stocking faster-moving flavours.

5- Amul Chocolates
Demand for chocolates was low in most outlets. Products such as Almond Bar Chocolate and Dark Chocolate had limited movement. In addition, stock unavailability was observed, as the salesman application showed zero stock for certain chocolate products, resulting in missed sales opportunities.

6- Amul Fresh Cream
Fresh Cream showed good retailer interest. However, due to the shorter shelf life of the product, retailers remained cautious about ordering larger quantities because of the risk of expiry and stock loss.

7- Price Sensitivity in Market
Although awareness of Amul products was high across the beat, several retailers mentioned that product prices are comparatively high for the local market. This pricing concern affected order conversion, especially in outlets serving price-sensitive customers.

8- Amul Atta Stock Issue
The ₹50 Amul Atta pack was reported to have demand in the market, but stock was unavailable. This represented a missed sales opportunity, as retailers were interested but unable to place orders.

9- Amul Biscuits & Cookies
Market feedback on biscuits was mixed. Some retailers indicated that Amul biscuits have demand in selected outlets, but broader retailer confidence remained low due to slower sales movement. Cookies also showed weak demand except in a few shops.

10- Mithai Mate & Premium Products
Products such as Mithai Mate, cheese, and paneer showed occasional demand, mainly in selected outlets. These categories appear to perform better in outlets with customers having relatively stronger purchasing capacity.

11- Non-ordering Outlets
Eight outlets did not place orders during the visit. The likely reasons included sufficient existing stock, slow movement of certain products, pricing concerns, and weak confidence in slower-moving categories.

Day 3 Comparative Analysis

Compared to previous field visits, Day 3 (Sarat Pally beat) showed a moderate performance with a 63.6% order conversion rate, which is lower than Day 1 (69.2%) and significantly below the National Highway beat performance (83.3%). One major external factor affecting Day 3 performance was rainfall, which reduced market activity, customer footfall, and overall product movement, making some retailers less willing to place fresh orders.

Despite this, core dairy products such as Amul Tazza and Amul Gold continued to show strong and consistent demand, confirming their importance across different beats. Compared to Sonai Road, Sarat Pally showed better potential for products like Amulya milk powder and Amul Kool, while the Tru beverage range showed only average performance, with Mango performing relatively better.

Another important challenge on Day 3 was stock unavailability for demand-driven products such as Amul Atta and chocolates, resulting in missed sales opportunities. Overall, Sarat Pally appears to be a price-sensitive market where essential dairy products perform reliably, but premium and slower-moving categories face resistance, especially under weaker market conditions caused by rain.

[26/05/26, 5:30:08 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: **Competitor Observation – Purabi vs Amul Milk & Lassi**

During market observation and customer interaction, Purabi showed a strong competitive presence in both the milk and lassi categories.

Based on retailer and customer feedback, the key reasons for preference toward Purabi over Amul are:

**1- Price Advantage in Lassi**
Retailers confirmed that **Purabi Lassi is priced at ₹17.5 compared to Amul Lassi at ₹18**, giving Purabi a slight pricing advantage. In a price-sensitive market, even a small difference can influence retailer stocking preference as well as customer purchase decisions.

**2- Price Difference in Milk**
Customers highlighted a noticeable price gap in the milk category. **Purabi 500 ml milk (plastic pack) is priced at ₹35, whereas Amul 500 ml tetra pack milk is priced at ₹40.** This ₹5 difference significantly impacts customer buying decisions, especially for a daily-use essential product like milk.

**3- Affordability Over Shelf Life**
Although **Purabi milk has a shorter shelf life of approximately 3 days due to plastic packaging**, many customers still prefer it because milk is purchased and consumed daily. This makes affordability a more important deciding factor than longer shelf life.

**4- Product Thickness & Taste Preference**
Some customers specifically mentioned that **Purabi Lassi is thicker in consistency compared to Amul Lassi**, which creates a perception of better quality, richer taste, and better value for money. This was identified as an important reason for customer preference in the lassi category.

**5- Taste & Familiarity Preference**
Several customers and retailers indicated that they are more familiar with Purabi products and are accustomed to their taste. Regular consumption over time has built stronger trust and comfort with the brand.

**6- Local Brand Advantage**
Purabi, being a regional brand, enjoys stronger local acceptance and customer familiarity, especially in regular dairy categories where habitual buying behaviour is common.

**Conclusion:**
Purabi’s competitive advantage appears to be driven by **better affordability in both milk and lassi, thicker lassi consistency with better perceived taste, strong local brand familiarity, and established customer trust.** Despite Amul offering longer shelf life through tetra packaging, customers in this market currently seem to prioritize **price, taste, and daily convenience over packaging advantages.**
[26/05/26, 5:38:59 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Distributor Name: Sengupta Agencies

Intern Name: Tahir Choudhury

Outlets Visited: 17
Orders Received: 13

Field Observations-

1- Amul Tazza & Amul Gold
Amul Tazza and Amul Gold showed very strong demand across the N S Avenue beat. Among all SKUs, Amul Tazza 200ml emerged as the highest-moving product, with significant bulk ordering from multiple outlets. Amul Gold also maintained steady movement, confirming strong daily consumer demand for fresh milk products in this market.

2- High Demand for Small Pack Sizes
Smaller pack sizes, such as 200 ml Tazza and 200 ml Gold, performed exceptionally well. This indicates a strong preference among consumers for affordable, daily-use pack sizes, likely influenced by budget-conscious purchasing behaviour.

3- Strong Performance in Value Outlets
Certain outlets, such as Mridula Store, Rimjim Enterprise, and Malay Trading, generated high-value orders with multiple product categories. These outlets demonstrated strong product movement and represent key growth points for repeat business.

4- Lassi Demand & Competitive Pressure
Amul Lassi showed good demand in several outlets. However, competitive pressure from Purabi was clearly visible in some stores, especially Aman Enterprise, where retailers indicated stronger customer preference for Purabi products due to taste and perceived quality advantages.

5- Grocery Product Performance
Products such as Amulya, Amulya Spray, biscuits, butter, paneer, and cheese showed positive market response. Amulya and Amulya Spray particularly had stronger retailer demand, suggesting good acceptance in grocery-focused outlets.

6- Biscuits & Value-Added Products
Biscuits showed moderate to good demand in selected outlets. Almond biscuits and chocolate biscuits moved into some stores, but overall biscuit movement remained outlet-dependent. Value-added products such as cheese slices, fresh cream, butter, and paneer also showed selective but healthy demand.

7- Delivery & Service Issue
At Connekxion, the retailer expressed frustration due to the delayed delivery of products. Such operational issues negatively affect retailer confidence and may impact future order conversion if unresolved.

8- Price Sensitivity & Month-End Spending Behaviour
Market conditions reflected noticeable price sensitivity. Retailers mentioned reduced customer spending due to month-end cash constraints. For example, Store 1 placed a smaller order due to slower market spending activity.

9- Low Market Footfall Impact
Several retailers reported lower customer footfall, leading to cautious ordering behaviour. High-value outlets were particularly conservative due to uncertainty in consumer spending patterns.

10- Stock Availability in Non-ordering Outlets
Some outlets, such as Kala Chand Bahandar, did not place orders due to sufficient existing stock, especially in milk products. This indicates inventory saturation rather than a lack of product demand.

11- Non-ordering Outlets
Four outlets did not place orders during the visit. The likely reasons included sufficient stock availability, low customer footfall, month-end spending constraints, competitive pressure, and cautious retailer sentiment due to slower market movement.
[26/05/26, 5:39:30 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Day 4 Comparative Analysis

Compared to previous field visits, Day 4 (N S Avenue beat) delivered the strongest performance so far with a 76.5% order conversion rate, improving over Day 1 (69.2%) and Day 3 (63.6%), while remaining slightly below the National Highway beat (83.3%).

Core dairy products, especially Amul Tazza and Amul Gold, continued to show strong demand, with particularly high movement in smaller pack sizes like 200 ml Tazza and Gold, indicating strong daily consumption demand. Compared to previous beats, Day 4 also showed better cross-category sales, with good movement in lassi, butter, paneer, cheese, biscuits, and fresh cream.

High-value outlets such as Mridula Store, Rimjim Enterprise, and Malay Trading contributed significantly to sales performance.

However, month-end spending pressure, low market footfall, competitive pressure from Purabi, and delayed delivery issues affected some outlets and retailer confidence.

Overall, N S Avenue emerged as a strong-performing beat with high dairy demand and better cross-selling opportunities, though competition and operational challenges remain areas of concern.

9. Amul chocolate sales is observed to be low in this beat compared to other beats although a few outlets (B. Sengupta, Radharani store, and Paul bhandar) ordered bindas. Apparently, demand for other chocolates like dark chocolate, fruit and nut, chocomini and other amul chocolates are low in this beat.

[27/05/26, 8:03:15 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Distributor Name: Sengupta Agencies
Beat Name: Hailakandi Road
Salesman Name: Sushil Das
Intern Name: Tahir Choudhury
Total No. of Outlets: 22
Outlets Visited: 22
Orders Received: 17
Order Conversion Rate: 77.3%

Field Observations

1- Amul Tazza & Amul Gold
Amul Tazza and Amul Gold showed exceptionally strong demand across the Hailakandi Road beat. Among all SKUs, Amul Tazza 200 ml recorded the highest movement, indicating strong daily household consumption and preference for affordable, smaller pack sizes. Multiple outlets placed bulk repeat orders for these products, making fresh milk the strongest sales driver in this market.

2- Strong Demand for Value-Added Dairy Products
Products such as paneer, fresh cream, cheese slices, butter, Mithai Mate, and ghee showed strong movement across multiple outlets. Frozen paneer in particular showed excellent demand in selected stores, indicating strong acceptance of premium dairy products in this market.

3- Amulya & Amulya Spray Performance
Amulya milk powder and Amulya Spray showed strong retailer acceptance. Demand was observed for ₹10 strips, 100 g packs, 200 g packs, and 500 g packs, indicating healthy movement in grocery-linked dairy categories.

4- Beverage Category Performance
Products such as Amul Masti, Lassi, Amul Kool, and the Amul Tru beverage range showed positive demand in this beat. Among Tru variants, Mango and Litchi showed better acceptance, suggesting growing consumer demand for ready-to-drink beverages, especially during warmer weather.

5- Strong Biscuit & Bakery Product Presence
Amul biscuits, cookies, Butter Nankhatai, Butter Cookies, Chocolate Cookies, and T-Special Rusk were visibly present in multiple outlets. Retailer stocking patterns suggest strong acceptance of Amul’s bakery and snacking products compared to some previous beats.

6- Chocolate Category Opportunity
Amul chocolates showed better market presence in this beat. Products were stocked in visible counter display areas in selected stores, indicating impulse purchase potential and moderate consumer demand.

7- Strong Multi-Category Brand Penetration
Field observations confirmed Amul’s strong presence across multiple categories including:

fresh milk
beverages
grocery dairy
premium dairy
frozen products
sweets
bakery/snacking

This indicates that Amul has strong brand penetration in this beat beyond traditional dairy products.

8- High-Potential Outlets
Several outlets in the Hailakandi Road beat were identified as high-potential outlets due to strong order volumes, wider product assortment, and stronger retailer confidence in Amul products.

Key high-potential outlets identified:

Basudev Bhander
Suman Store
Budget Foods
Adhunik Shop
Radharaman Bhandar
Juytika Stores

These outlets showed:

higher order quantities
broader multi-category product acceptance
stronger premium product movement
better retailer willingness to stock multiple SKUs

Examples:

Basudev Bhander placed one of the highest milk volume orders along with premium dairy products.
Radharaman Bhandar showed strong movement in milk, paneer, beverages, and premium dairy.
Suman Store demonstrated balanced demand across milk, ghee, butter, cheese, and grocery-linked dairy products.
Budget Foods showed acceptance across beverages, dairy, paneer, sweets, and premium products.
Adhunik Shop showed strong grocery-category movement, including Amulya, butter, spray products, and chocolates.
Juytika Stores maintained a broad product assortment, indicating strong consumer pull.

These outlets can be treated as priority accounts for deeper product penetration and new SKU expansion.

9- Organic Product Demand Challenge
Amul organic products showed weak market acceptance in this beat. At Basudev Bhander, expired Amul organic stock was physically observed, and the retailer specifically reported poor movement of organic products.

Likely reasons include:

Higher pricing compared to competing alternatives
lower consumer awareness
price sensitivity in the local market

This category appears unsuitable for aggressive distribution in this beat.

10- Damaged & Expired Product Concern
At Basudev Bhander, the retailer raised concerns regarding damaged and expired Amul products. Physical verification confirmed:

expired organic stock
damaged liquid carton packs
expiry in select premium products

This indicates:

inventory rotation challenges
possible overstocking of slow-moving SKUs
retailer dissatisfaction due to replacement and wastage concerns

11- Visual Merchandising Opportunity
Although Amul products had strong availability across outlets, some stores displayed products in partially obstructed or less visible shelf spaces.

Improvement in:

shelf visibility
product placement
cold storage organization

could further improve impulse purchases and category sales.

12- Premium Product Acceptance
Products such as:

paneer
fresh cream
cheese
sweets
ghee

showed stronger movement in selected outlets, suggesting that premium Amul products perform well where customer purchasing power is relatively higher.

13- Price Sensitivity in Premium Categories
Although core dairy products showed strong movement, premium-priced categories such as organic products faced resistance in price-sensitive outlets. Retailers showed a stronger preference for faster-moving, affordable SKUs.

14- Non-Ordering Outlets
Five outlets did not place orders during the visit:

Abhiruchi Store
Cold Bar
Maa Pan Store
Gopal Stores
New Govindo Store

Likely reasons included:

sufficient existing stock
slow movement of certain categories
low immediate product requirement
mismatch of outlet demand with offered SKUs

For example, Abhiruchi Store had recently ordered T-Rusk and reported a positive response, but no fresh order was placed during this visit.

[30/05/26, 10:37:46 PM] Biprashi Nath Corporate: Kalain Market Observation Report
Date: 30 May 2026
Location: Kalain Market and Nearby Localities
Intern: Tahir Choudhury and Biprarshi Nath 

Market Observation

- During the market visit, the Amul products commonly observed were Amul Taaza, Amul Gold, Amul Butter, Amulya, and Amul Spray.
- Amul Cool was rarely available in retail outlets, indicating low product visibility and availability.
- Kalain is a highly price-sensitive market, and customers generally prefer products that offer lower prices or better value for money.
- Compared to competitor brands, the overall visibility and presence of Amul products in the market were significantly lower.
- Several retailers mentioned that they are reluctant to stock Amul products due to comparatively lower profit margins.
- Retailers, distributors, and salesmen highlighted issues related to low product availability and irregular supply.
- Salesmen reported that poor road conditions and connectivity issues make product distribution difficult, affecting stock availability in the market.
- Many shopkeepers stated that Kalain has a limited distributor presence, and several retailers depend on distributors operating from Badarpur for product supply.
- Due to Eid celebrations, market activity was relatively slow, and customer footfall was lower than usual during the visit.
- Competitor brands enjoyed better shelf presence, wider availability, and stronger retailer acceptance.

Stores Visited :
R.U.Store, Jay Guru Store, Giyas Uddin Store, Bhumat Commercial , Kha Store, A.K. Store, 

Market Observations 

1.Across the visited outlets in Kalain Market, retailers regularly stocked and ordered products such as Amul Butter, Amul Spray, Amul Gold, Mithai Mate, and Amul T-Special. The consistent presence and replenishment of these products indicate steady consumer demand and strong retailer confidence in Amul's core product range within the market.

2.While pitching Amul Paneer, several retailers stated that they currently sell competing brands such as Prayag, Red Cow, and Ajanta Paneer. Retailers highlighted that these brands offer better trade margins, with one retailer purchasing Prayag Paneer at ₹91 and selling it at ₹100, while another purchased Ajanta Paneer at ₹103 and sold it between ₹110–115.Some retailers also expressed difficulty in selling paneer at the printed MRP, which affects their willingness to stock higher-priced brands.

3.Retailers mentioned that they had previously stocked Amul Paneer but experienced limited consumer demand. According to them, customers in the market are highly price-sensitive and generally prefer lower-priced alternatives. 

4.The owner of R.U. Store stated that he had placed an order for Amul Lassi but did not receive the product, indicating a supply gap at the outlet.

5.According to the distributor, while Amul Organic products are of good quality, demand is limited as customers in the market tend to prioritize price over premium product attributes. 

6.Competing brands demonstrated stronger market visibility, broader product availability, and greater retailer preference across the visited outlets.

7.Retailers, distributors, and sales representatives pointed out concerns regarding inconsistent product availability and supply-related challenges in the market

[01/06/26, 10:21:39 PM] Tahir NIT Silchar: Distributor Visit Observation Report
Date: 30 May 2026
Location: Kalain and Nearby Localities
Intern: Tahir Choudhury and Biprarshi Nath

### New Distributor Observation

* During the visit, we interacted with a new distributor to understand the market potential for Amul edible oil and other Amul products in rural and semi-urban markets.
* The distributor’s godown was well maintained, and proper cleanliness and hygiene standards were observed.
* Separate godowns were maintained for different product categories and brands such as Parle Agro, Haldiram, and Nestlé.
* A small sample shop was also maintained for displaying products and interacting with retailers.
* The distributor supplies products in areas such as Digorkhal, Jalalpur, Gumra, and Kalain.
* The distributor showed interest not only in Amul edible oil but also in other Amul products.
* The distributor mentioned that the demand for refined oil is comparatively higher than that of mustard oil in this market, due to which he showed greater interest in refined oil products.
* Discussions mainly focused on retailer margins, market demand, stock movement, and future business opportunities.

### Existing Distributor Observation

* We also interacted with an existing distributor to understand the actual market conditions and consumer behavior in the edible oil segment.
* According to the distributor, the edible oil market mainly depends on retailer schemes, bargaining margins, and attractive deals offered to retailers.
* The distributor stated that convincing customers is the biggest challenge in this market, as consumers mainly focus on affordability rather than health benefits.
* The market was observed to be highly price sensitive, where customers prefer lower-priced products offering better value for money.
* The distributor explained that healthier oils and organic products are often considered premium or luxury products by local consumers.
* He compared Amul edible oil and organic-category products with luxury brands, which are mainly affordable to higher-income customers.
* According to the distributor, such premium products are currently not very suitable for this particular market area due to low purchasing preference for high-priced healthy products.
* Customer buying decisions were observed to be mostly influenced by pricing rather than nutritional value or product quality.
* The interaction provided valuable insights regarding rural consumer behavior, distributor operations, pricing sensitivity, and the challenges faced while promoting premium FMCG products in cost-conscious markets.

Observations:

1. Price Sensitivity Among Retailers and Consumers
Retailers reported that consumers in the beat area prefer Pran Rusk due to its comparatively lower price.
For example, the MRP of Pran Rusk was ₹70, whereas Amul T-Special Rusk and other cookie variants were priced higher, affecting sales preference.

2. Near-Expiry Product Supply Issue
Some retailers reported receiving products with very short remaining shelf life. This negatively impacted retailer confidence and affected order generation during the visit.

3. Milkshake Margin Comparison
A retailer mentioned that the margin on Amul Milkshake is Rs.22.25 (PTR basis), while Purabi Milkshake offers a margin of Rs.21.25. Retailers are comparing margins across brands while making stocking decisions.

4. Complaint Regarding Amul Tin Paneer
A retailer reported that paneer cubes inside Amul Tin Paneer containers were found broken upon opening. This quality concern affected customer satisfaction and repeat purchases. 

5. Demand for Amul Kool Kesar Badam Milkshake
A retailer specifically requested Amul Kool Kesar Badam Milkshake and Amul Kool in plastic bottle packaging. Those products were out of stock, leading to unmet retailer demand.

6. High Demand Products
Strong sales and demand were observed for:
Amulya Milk Powder
Amul Tru beverages

7. Visibility and Sales of Amul Masti and Lassi
Amul Masti and Amul Lassi were visible across multiple outlets. Retailers reported regular sales of these products. 

8. Damage Product Replacement Concern
A Das Store reported dissatisfaction regarding the non-replacement of damaged products received earlier.
The retailer stated that future orders would be placed only after replacement and refund of the damaged stock.

9. Consumer Preference in Rural Market
Since the beat covered a predominantly rural area, affordable products witnessed higher demand.
Premium beverages and higher-priced cookie variants showed relatively low movement. Despite this, efforts were made to generate orders for premium products where possible.

10. Some retailers requested branded chocolate storage/display boxes similar to those provided by competing brands. Retailers highlighted storage challenges and felt that dedicated storage boxes would improve product handling, visibility, and sales.

11. Amul Ghee was observed to be sold in 1–2 outlets, indicating a presence in the market and steady demand among a limited number of retailers. ‎<This message was edited>

[02/06/26, 8:30:19 PM] Prakash Rakshit MBA: Katlicherra Market 
Intern Name - Prakash Jyoti Rakshit 
Today's Market Observations :

1. Strong Availability of Core Amul Products

Most retailers stocked Amulya, Mithai Mate, Amul Butter, Amul Taaza, Amul Gold, and Amul Spray. These products have consistent consumer demand and are considered fast-moving items, making them a priority for retailers.

2. High Demand for Milk Products

Amul Gold and Amul Taaza were available in most outlets visited. Their strong presence indicates regular household consumption and steady daily demand, making milk one of the most important categories for retailers.

3. Popularity of Dairy Whiteners

Amulya and Mithai Mate were commonly stocked across outlets. Their popularity is driven by usage in tea, coffee, and sweet preparations, leading to frequent purchases by both households and commercial customers.

4. Good Market Acceptance of Butter

Amul Butter was visible in many shops and appeared to be a preferred product. Strong brand recognition, customer trust, and regular household usage contribute to its consistent demand in the market.

5. Presence of Tea Special ₹10 Pack

Some retailers stocked Tea Special ₹10 sachets, indicating demand for affordable and convenient pack sizes. Such products appeal to price-sensitive consumers and tea vendors who prefer smaller purchase quantities.

6. Low Demand for Amul Ghee

Retailers reported that Amul Ghee experiences lower customer preference and demand compared to other dairy products. Its higher price and less frequent purchase cycle may contribute to slower sales turnover.

7. Limited Availability of Fresh Cream

Only three outlets were found stocking Fresh Cream during the survey. This suggests either limited consumer demand, insufficient retailer interest, or inadequate distribution coverage within the surveyed market area.

8. Low Visibility of Amul Biscuits

Amul biscuits were observed in only a few outlets. Compared to dairy products, the biscuit category appears to have lower market penetration and may require stronger distribution and promotional support.

9. Retailer Complaint Regarding T-Special Rusk

One retailer reported receiving damaged T-Special Rusk products. This issue may be related to transportation, storage, or handling practices and could negatively affect retailer satisfaction and future orders.

10. Strong Competition in Paneer Segment

Ajanta, Prayag, and Red Cow paneer were available in many outlets. Retailers stated that customers frequently demand these brands, resulting in stronger shelf presence and sales compared to competing paneer products.
[02/06/26, 9:55:52 PM] Rohan Dam MBA: Date :- 02/06/2026
Location :- Badarpur Market 
Intern name :- Rohan Kumar Dam

Stores Visited :
Lokenath Bhandar, New Laxmi Bhandar, Shankar Bhandar, Ahmed Traders, Pritam Dey Store, Mahadev Store, Needs a store of varities 

Market Observations 

Atta Category – Market Insights and Competitor Analysis

1.During the visit to Badarpur Market, Aashirvaad Atta and Fortune Atta emerged as the leading brands in the atta category. These products were available across most of the visited outlets, and retailers consistently reported regular sales, reflecting strong market penetration, consumer acceptance, and retailer confidence in these brands.

2.Retailers reported that the 1 kg and 5 kg pack sizes account for the majority of atta sales in the market, indicating stronger consumer preference for these pack variants and higher sales movement compared to other pack sizes.

3.Retailers reported receiving trade margins ranging from 7–8% to 10% on Aashirvaad Atta and Fortune Atta, making these brands commercially attractive and contributing to their strong retailer acceptance in the market.

4.The owner of Shankar Bhandar stated that promotional schemes, such as the "Buy One Get One Free" offer previously introduced by Fortune Atta, have helped strengthen the brand's market presence and enhance its visibility among consumers.

5.Retailers reported purchasing Aashirvaad Atta 1 kg packs at a PTR of approximately ₹52–53 and selling them at ₹60.One retailer reported selling loose (non-packaged) atta at approximately ₹36 per kg, with customers continuing to purchase the product due to its lower price point.

6.While discussing Amul Atta with retailers, one retailer mentioned that the product offers comparatively lower margins than competing atta brands, which influences stocking preferences at the outlet level.

Paneer Category – Market Insights and Retailer Feedback

1.In the paneer category, Top, Red Cow, Bikaji, Parag and Ajanta Paneer were available across the visited outlets. Among these brands, Red Cow demonstrated the strongest market presence and was the most prominently stocked paneer brand in the market.

2.The owner of Pritam Dey Store stated that, in his opinion, the taste and quality of Red Cow Paneer are not superior; however, customers continue to purchase the brand due to their familiarity and established buying habits. 

3.Retailers reported purchasing Parag Paneer at approximately ₹95 and selling it at ₹110. For Red Cow Paneer, retailers cited purchase prices ranging from ₹93–102 and selling prices of around ₹110. One retailer also noted a recent increase of ₹2–3 in the purchase price of Red Cow Paneer, indicating variations in procurement costs across outlets.

4.The owner of Mahadev Store stated that he had previously stocked Amul Paneer; however, consumer demand for the product was limited, resulting in lower sales movement at the outlet.The owner also stated that while the quality of Amul Paneer is good, customers in the market are highly price-sensitive and generally prefer lower-priced alternatives over premium-quality products.

Lassi & Amul Kool Category Analysis

1.Mother Dairy Lassi had good shelf presence in the visited outlet. The retailer stated that he prefers stocking the product due to its comparatively better margins, purchasing it at ₹17 and selling it at ₹20.

2.Retailers showed a positive response towards Amul Kool, and the product maintained good availability and visibility across the visited outlets. The owner of Mahadev Store stated that he earns approximately ₹80 after selling one case of Amul Kool.

Consumer preference for Amul Kool varied across markets. While PET bottles were more popular in the previous beat, retailers in today's market indicated stronger demand for the glass bottle variant.

Market Observations – Gumra Market
1- Demand for Promotional Materials

Retailers demanded promotional materials such as posters and banners to improve product visibility and customer awareness inside stores. Better visual merchandising may help improve product movement in the market.

2- Strong Demand for Affordable SKUs

Retailers showed stronger interest in smaller and more affordable product packs. Mehbub Stores specifically demanded small biscuit packs, indicating a higher consumer preference for low-price, daily consumption products in this market.

3- Beverage Category Performance

Products such as Amul Lassi, Amul Masti, Amul Kool, Badam Shake, and the Amul Tru beverage range showed positive demand in the Gumra market. Beverage products performed strongly in selected outlets, especially larger retail stores.

Among Tru variants:

Mango and Lychee showed better retailer acceptance.

At Paul Store, the existing stock of 3–4 cases of Amul Cool glass bottles was observed, indicating the existing market presence of Amul beverages.

4- Strong Bakery Product Movement

Amul T-Rusk (Toast) showed strong movement across multiple outlets and emerged as one of the most accepted products during the visit. Retailers placed orders for both cartons and loose pieces, indicating regular customer demand in the bakery/snacking segment.

5- Paneer Demand with Strong Price Sensitivity

Amul Paneer showed demand in several outlets, including Barbhuiya Store, Radhakrishna Store, New Barbhuiya Store, and Pulok Das Store. However, several retailers raised concerns regarding the higher price of Amul Paneer compared to competing brands.

Non-ordering retailers specifically mentioned that competitor paneer brands are being sold in the market at approximately ₹97–₹99, whereas Amul Paneer is priced around ₹106. Due to this price difference, many retailers showed hesitation in placing fresh paneer orders and preferred lower-priced competing alternatives.

This indicates strong price sensitivity in the paneer category within the Gumra market, particularly among smaller retail outlets.

6- Competitive Brand Presence

Mother Dairy products were observed to have strong market dominance in several outlets. Winkies Lassi also showed strong market presence and customer preference in the beverage segment.

Britannia Lassi additionally showed good shelf presence across multiple retail outlets, indicating strong competition in the ready-to-drink dairy beverage category. Humans really turned flavored milk into a battlefield of shelf politics and refrigeration strategy.

7- Limited Demand for Selected Premium Products

Amul Kaju Katli showed limited market demand in this beat. Retailers appeared more interested in fast-moving daily consumption products compared to premium sweet categories.

8- Chocolate Category Observation

Amul chocolates were pitched in multiple outlets during the visit, but retailers did not place orders due to comparatively lower demand and stronger preference for faster-moving products.

Observations:

1. High Demand, Low Availability: Products such as Amul Butter (50g), Almond Nankhatai, Amul T-Special Rusk, T-Special Milk (₹10 sachet), and Fresh Cream showed strong demand but were frequently out of stock.
2. Low Awareness of Organic Products: Retailers have limited awareness of organic products and therefore do not prioritize stocking them. Warehouse stock availability is also inadequate.
3. Challenges in Organic Product Sales: Retailers cited low consumer awareness, higher prices, and lower margins as key reasons for not keeping organic products.
4. Short-Expiry Stock Issue: Fresh cream with a short remaining shelf life was supplied to two retailers—Gobindo Das and Jadab Chandra Das—creating potential inventory and sales concerns.
5. Demand for Peanut Butter: A retailer specifically requested peanut butter, but the product was out of stock, indicating existing market visibility and demand.
6. Strong Beverage Sales: Beverage products demonstrated good movement and sales performance across the market.
7. Pricing Concerns: Retailers reported difficulty selling products priced at unconventional points such as ₹14 or ₹22. Round-figure pricing is preferred as customers often pay only ₹20, making transactions inconvenient and reducing retailer interest in stocking such products.

[08/06/26, 9:27:50 PM] Manjeet Nath Laskar MBA: Date: 06-06-2026

Distributor Name - Jagannath Enterprise.


Intern Name - Manjeet Nath Laskar


Outlets visited: 5


Task: Competitor analysis 


Paneer: 

*Amul*
*210g:* Retails at ₹79 (PTR: ₹66.52). This translates to ₹376.19 per kg, offering the retailer a 15.8% margin.
*425g:* Retails at ₹135 (PTR: ₹105.67). This translates to ₹317.65 per kg, offering the retailer a massive 21.7% margin.
*800g:* Retails at ₹264 (PTR: ₹222.29). This translates to ₹330.00 per kg, offering the retailer a 15.8% margin.

*Redcow*
*200g:* Retails at ₹65 (PTR: ₹55.00). This translates to ₹325.00 per kg, offering the retailer a 15.4% margin.
*Shyam (Sunrise) & Gopal*
Both brands price their *200g* SKU identically: Retailing at ₹62 (PTR: ₹55.00). This translates to ₹310.00 per kg, offering the retailer a much lower 11.3% margin.

*Bikaji*
*400g:* Retails at ₹110 (PTR: ₹90.00). This translates to ₹275.00 per kg, offering the retailer an 18.2% margin.
*1kg:* Retails at ₹180 (PTR: ₹167.00). This translates to a highly aggressive ₹180.00 per kg, offering the retailer a razor-thin 7.2% margin.

*Takeaways*
Where most retailers are acquiring similar variant products from local wholesaler at a slightly inflated price than Agency, it still cost them less than Amul. This profit margin gap discourages retailers to sell amul malai paneer. 
Amul offers the highest consumer price in the market (up to ₹376 per kg in case of 200g pack) but backs this up by offering the highest channel margins (peaking at 21.7% for the 425g SKU) to ensure retailers actively stock and promote their product. 


Rusk: 

*Amul (Tea Special)*
*200g:* Retails at ₹35 (PTR: ₹30.10). Offering the retailer a 14.0% margin.

*Ruskit*
*200g:* Retails at ₹35 (PTR: ₹31.00). Offering the retailer an 11.4% margin.

*Takeaways*
Amul has priced its Rusk exactly the same as Ruskit for the end consumer. However, Amul’s lower PTR gives the retailer an extra 2.6% margin. In fast-moving consumer goods, this slight margin advantage at identical consumer price points is a textbook strategy to win retailer preference.
Hoever not all retailers were holding amul rusk SKUs due to low availability and lack of product awareness.


Refined Cooking Oil: 

*Fortune*
*1L:* Retails at ₹165 (PTR: ₹147.00), offering the retailer a 10.9% margin.

*Mahakosh*
*1L:* Retails at ₹145 (PTR: ₹133.00), offering the retailer an 8.3% margin.

*Independence*
*1L:* Retails at ₹180 (PTR: ₹168.00), offering the retailer a 6.7% margin.

*Janmay*
*1L:* Retails at ₹150 (PTR not known).

*Strategic Takeaways*
Fortune dominates the channel strategy due to huge market presence and habitual buying. It maintains a premium consumer price point (₹165) while offering the best margin in the category (10.9%). 
Independence is the most expensive oil on the shelf at ₹180 per liter, yet offers the lowest profit margin to the retailer (6.7%). 


Ghee: 

*Manorama*
*150ml:* Retails at ₹220 (PTR: ₹198.00), offering the retailer a 10% margin. 

*Amul Ghee*
*200ml:* Retails at ₹132. 

*Takeaways*
Normalizing these volumes shows they are not competing in the same segment. Amul's pricing sits comfortably in the mass-market dairy ghee category. Manorama, at over double the per-liter price, is positioned as an ultra-premium, niche, or specialty product where standard fast-moving retail rules do not apply.

Market Observation Point:

1. Despite the absence of the salesman during the market visit, retailer interactions were conducted independently to gather market insights, assess product availability, and understand customer demand patterns across the assigned outlets.


2. Most retailers reported that orders would be communicated directly to the salesman through phone calls or WhatsApp, indicating that remote order collection is commonly practiced in this beat.


3. Amul Taaza and Amul Gold continued to maintain strong visibility across the majority of outlets, reflecting their position as the most regularly stocked and demanded dairy products.


4. Several outlets were carrying adequate stock levels, suggesting that retailers are maintaining inventory cautiously and placing replenishment orders based on product movement and demand.


5. Competitor brands remained visible in multiple outlets, particularly in the paneer, biscuit, and beverage categories, indicating continued market competition and customer preference for a variety of product options.


6. Retailer discussions highlighted the importance of timely order collection and product availability, as delayed communication or stock shortages can impact product replenishment and sales opportunities.


7. The visit provided an opportunity to focus on market observation, product visibility, competitor presence, and retailer feedback, helping identify areas for improving product penetration and availability in the beat.


8. Overall market activity appeared normal, with retailers continuing regular business operations while relying on both physical visits and digital communication channels for order placement and product inquiries.

Market observations

Most retailers reported a significant decline in sales due to the ongoing summer vacation at NIT Silchar. Since students are not attending the campus regularly, footfall in nearby stores has reduced considerably, resulting in lower sales.

Despite the slowdown, there is good scope for chocolates, sweets, organic items and cookies in stores located near the NIT campus.

Among sweets, Kaju Katli and Lal Peda were found to have strong demand.

In the cookies category, T Special Rusk, Butter Toast, Almond Nankhatai, and Cashew Nankhatai showed higher demand.

Among chocolates, Fruit Nut Chocolate both 40g and 150g bars emerged as a high-demand product, along with Amul Velvet Chocolate. Sugar-Free Dark Chocolate and Dark Chocolate both 75g and 150g bars continue to have moderate to good demand.

In the beverages segment, Amul Kool Café (both plastic and glass bottle variants) recorded high demand. Amul Kool Café Can showed moderate demand, while Amul Kool Badam registered strong demand in this beat.

In the organic product category, Organic Atta and Organic Sugar were in high demand across most outlets.

Amul Lassi (150 ml pack) also demonstrated strong demand in the market.

Out of the 15 outlets visited, orders were placed at 6 outlets only. The lower ordering trend indicates a significant reduction in retailers' replenishment requirements, primarily due to decreased sales during the NIT summer vacation period. As overall sales have declined, retailers have reduced their order quantities and ordering frequency accordingly.

Several retailers expressed demand for products such as Bindass Chocolate, Amul Butter (₹35 pack), Amul Masti Dahi, and Lal Peda. However, orders for these items could not be placed as they were unavailable due to stock shortages. The unavailability of these products resulted in missed sales opportunities in the visited outlets.

Intern Name - Prakash Jyoti Rakshit 
Salesman -Suman Dhar
No. Of outlets visited - 18

Market Observation Points :

1. Products ordered by retailers during the previous day were successfully delivered to the respective outlets today. The delivery process ensured timely stock replenishment, helping retailers maintain product availability and meet ongoing customer demand for regularly purchased dairy products.


2. Payment collection was carried out from multiple retail outlets during the visit. While some retailers cleared their outstanding payments in full, others made partial payments and assured that the remaining amount would be settled in subsequent visits. This reflects varying cash flow situations among retailers while maintaining continued business relations.


3. Retailers who received fresh stock reported satisfactory movement of core Amul products, particularly Taaza, Gold, Amulya, Butter, and Mithai Mate, which continue to drive regular sales in the market.


4. Several outlets were found to have reduced stock levels compared to the previous visit, indicating that products supplied earlier had moved reasonably well despite recent fluctuations in customer footfall.


5. Competitor brands such as Nescafé, Ajanta, Prayag, and Red Cow continued to be visible in several stores, indicating ongoing competition in beverage and paneer categories where retailers are actively comparing pricing and margins.


6. Retailers emphasized the importance of timely product delivery, as regular replenishment helps maintain product availability and prevents loss of sales opportunities, especially for fast-moving dairy products.


7. Overall, the market showed stable product movement and efficient distribution activity today. The focus was primarily on product delivery, stock replenishment, and payment recovery, providing a better understanding of distributor operations and retailer servicing processes.

Market Observation Points

1. During today's market visit, focus was given on pitching Amul Bindaaz Wafer, Dark Passion chocolates, and beverage products such as Amul Tru, Kool, and Lassi. Positive response was received from multiple retailers, resulting in successful order placements across different outlets.


2. Amul Bindaaz Wafer Choco received the strongest retailer response, with multiple outlets placing orders. Daily Mart and Deb Pan Corner showed comparatively higher interest by ordering larger quantities, indicating good potential for this product in general trade outlets.


3. Amul Dark Passion also received encouraging acceptance from retailers, with orders placed across multiple stores. The response indicates that chocolate products have potential for growth, especially when targeted towards impulse purchase locations like pan shops, cafes, and grocery stores.


4. Beverage products including Amul Kool and Amul Tru variants received positive responses from selected outlets. Retailers showed interest in stocking multiple flavours, indicating demand for ready-to-drink beverages, especially during the summer season.


5. Maa Durga Cafe showed good acceptance towards a wider range of products by ordering Bindaaz Wafer, Dark Passion, all three Tru flavours, Lassi, and Kool. Cafes and food outlets appear to be promising targets for beverage and snack category expansion.


6. Pan shops and high-footfall outlets showed better response towards chocolates and wafers due to their impulse purchase nature. These outlets can be considered important channels for increasing visibility and trial of new Amul snack products.


7. Retailer interest in new category products was positive compared to previous visits, where milk products were the primary demand drivers. This indicates an opportunity to improve penetration of Amul’s bakery, chocolate, and beverage segments in the market.


8. Regular follow-up with retailers after product placement will be important to track sales movement, collect feedback, and identify outlets with potential for repeat orders and wider product assortment.

9. Due to several retailers already placing orders during the previous day's visit, many outlets had sufficient stock available and did not place fresh orders during today's visit. Retailers showed interest but preferred to wait until the existing stock moved before placing additional orders, affecting the overall order quantity received today.

[12/06/26, 11:41:39 PM] Rohan Dam MBA: Date :- 12/06/2026

Intern name :- Rohan Kumar Dam

Edible Oil Category – Market Pricing, Retail Insights & Competitive Landscape

1.The edible oil category remains highly competitive, with multiple established brands such as Engine, Mahakosh, Tiger, Fortune, Mast Oil, King's, and Sreeji competing for market share across the visited outlets. Retailers reported that customer purchase decisions are primarily influenced by price, brand familiarity, and product availability.

2.Retailer interactions revealed that market selling prices for edible oils often differ from the printed MRP due to competitive pricing practices. For example, Engine Oil (1 litre) was reported to have a PTR of approximately ₹190–195 and an MRP of ₹210, while retailers typically sell it at around ₹205 to remain competitive in the market.

3.Retailers reported that Engine Oil (500 ml) is procured at approximately ₹64 against an MRP of ₹70, indicating a limited but steady margin structure within the category. Similar pricing patterns were observed across other leading edible oil brands.

4.Mahakosh Oil was reported to have a PTR ranging from ₹130–134 against an MRP of ₹138, while Fortune Oil was available with a PTR of approximately ₹146 against an MRP of ₹150. Retailers indicated that these brands maintain stable demand due to their established market presence and customer trust.

5.Tiger Oil continues to attract price-sensitive consumers due to its economical pricing. Retailers reported that the 1-litre pack has an MRP of ₹120 with a PTR of approximately ₹115, contributing to regular sales movement in the lower-price segment of the edible oil market.

Distribution Network Assessment & Retailer Outreach

1.Retailers in the Katigorah market area reported sourcing edible oil products primarily from distributors operating in Silchar and Badarpur. This indicates the market's dependence on nearby distribution networks for product supply and replenishment.

2.In addition to competitor analysis, retailers were approached to explore opportunities for expanding product distribution. Discussions were held to gather information on potential distributor contacts and assess prospects for strengthening market coverage in the area.
